(39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\right)\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Pr}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{J}=\left({\rm Id},\,\mathcal{K}_{3A0}\circ\Omega^{\lambda^{+}}\circ{\rm Pr}_{1}\right)}

(40)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{\rm model}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{l+2}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{m+2}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{-}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}^{\lambda^{-}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\lambda^{+}}\times B^{\lambda^{-}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\\
{\rm in\,\,(B0)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq m+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\\
{\rm in\,\,(B1)-(B3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq m+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\\
{\rm in\,\,(A0)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\\
{\rm in\,\,(A1)-(A3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}_{AB}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{AB}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{l+2},\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{m+2}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{2B0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\Omega^{\lambda^{+}},\,\Omega^{\lambda^{-}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{3B0}\circ{\rm Pr}_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{3B}\circ{\rm Pr}_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{B0}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{2B}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{1B}\circ{\rm pr}_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{2A0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{A0}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{3A0}\circ{\rm Pr}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Large{\cong}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{2A}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{1A}\circ{\rm pr}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}_{3A}\circ{\rm Pr}_{A}}

We proceed to establish (
P2
).

It is clear that
\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{l+2}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+}}\right)
is isomorphic to a closed subscheme of

(41)

\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}},

which as a topological space is the closure of the image in (
41
) under the rational map
\left({\rm Id},\mathcal{K}_{3A}\circ\Omega^{\lambda^{+}}\right)
from
\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}
to (
41
).

Denote by
{\rm Pr}_{1}
the projection from (
41
) to
\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}
. We can define a morphism

\mathcal{K}_{3A0}:B^{\lambda^{+}}\longrightarrow\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}\cong\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\\
{\rm in\,\,(A0)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}

by extending the rational map
\left(\cdots,\left.\left(F^{l}_{12i_{3}i_{4}}\circ e_{l}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}_{+}}\circ{\check{\mathcal{R}}}_{l+2}\right)\right|_{B^{\lambda^{+}}},\cdots\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}
. Then, there is an embedding from (
41
) to

(42)

\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}

defined by
\mathcal{J}:=\left({\rm Id},\mathcal{K}_{3A0}\circ\Omega^{\lambda^{+}}\circ{\rm Pr}_{1}\right)
.

Denote by
{\rm Pr}_{+}
the projection from (
42
) to

(43)

\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\right)\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}.

We claim that
{\rm Pr}_{+}\circ{\mathcal{J}}
is a locally closed embedding. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\lambda^{+}
is the identity permutation. Write the homogeneous coordinates for
\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}
as
\left[z_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{3}i_{2}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{4}i_{2}i_{3}}\right]
. Computation yields that

\begin{split}&\left({\rm Pr}_{+}\circ{\mathcal{J}}\right)\bigg(\left(a_{3},a_{4},\cdots,a_{l+2}\right),\left(\epsilon^{+}_{1}\right),\left(\epsilon^{+}_{2},\epsilon^{+}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{l}\right),\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left(\cdots,\left[z_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{3}i_{2}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{4}i_{2}i_{3}}\right],\cdots\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\right)\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,=\left(\left(a_{3},a_{4},\cdots,a_{l+2}\right),\left(\epsilon^{+}_{1}\right),\left(\cdots,\left[z_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{3}i_{2}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{4}i_{2}i_{3}}\right],\cdots\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}},\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.\left(\cdots,\left[\left(1-\prod\nolimits_{i_{3}-1}^{i_{4}-2}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right),1,\left(\prod\nolimits_{i_{3}-1}^{i_{4}-2}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right)\right],\cdots\right)_{3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2}\right).\\
\end{split}

By taking
(i_{3},i_{4})=(3,4),(4,5),\cdots,(l+1,l+2)
in

(44)

\left[\left(1-\prod\nolimits_{i_{3}-1}^{i_{4}-2}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right),1,\left(\prod\nolimits_{i_{3}-1}^{i_{4}-2}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right)\right],

we can conclude that
{\rm Pr}_{+}\circ{\mathcal{J}}
is a locally closed embedding.

Now by projecting (
43
) to

(45)

\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq l+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}\cong\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\,\,{\rm in}\\
{\rm(A0),\,(A1),\,(A2),\,(A3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}},

we can conclude that

(46)

\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{l+2}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\cong\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}.

Here
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}
is a locally closed subscheme of
(
45
) such that, as a topological space, it is the closure of the image in (
45
) under the rational map
\left(\mathcal{K}_{3A},\mathcal{K}_{3A0}\right)
. Similarly, we can conclude that

(47)

\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{m+2}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{-}}\right)\cong\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}},

where
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}
is a locally closed subscheme of

(48)

\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq m+2\\
(i_{1},i_{2})\neq(1,2)\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq m+2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{12i_{3}i_{4}}}\cong\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\,\,{\rm in}\\
{\rm(B0),\,(B1),\,(B2),\,(B3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}.

Moreover, according to the assumption that Proposition
3.1
holds for all
3\leq N^{\prime}<N
, we can derive that
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}
,
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}
are smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimension
l-1
,
m-1
, respectively.

Take integers
q_{A}\in\{1,2,\cdots,l\}
,
q_{B}\in\{1,2,\cdots,m\}
such that
\lambda^{+}(q_{A})=\lambda^{-}(q_{B})=1
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
j^{+}_{q_{A}}<j^{-}_{q_{B}}
. Computation yields that

(49)

\begin{split}&\left.\left(F_{12j^{+}_{q_{A}}j^{-}_{q_{B}}}\circ e\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{A0}\circ\mathcal{R}_{A0B0}\right)\right|_{B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}}\left(\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{E}_{+},\overrightarrow{E}_{-}\right)\\
&\,\,\,=\left[a_{j^{+}_{q_{A}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{q_{B}}}-a_{j^{+}_{q_{A}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{q_{B}}}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{+}\cdot\epsilon^{-}_{1},\,\,\,\,-a_{j^{+}_{q_{A}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{q_{B}}}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{+}\cdot\epsilon^{-}_{1},\,\,\,\,-a_{j^{+}_{q_{A}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{q_{B}}}\right]\\
&\,\,\,=\left[1-\epsilon_{1}^{+}\cdot\epsilon^{-}_{1},\,\,\,\,-\epsilon_{1}^{+}\cdot\epsilon^{-}_{1},\,\,\,\,-1\right],\\
\end{split}

which thus extends to a morphism. We can define a morphism
\mathcal{L}
from
\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)
to

(50)

U:=\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\,\,{\rm in}\\
{\rm(A0),\,(A1),\,(A2),\,(A3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\,\,{\rm in}\\
{\rm(B0),\,(B1),\,(B2),\,(B3)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}\times\mathbb{P}^{12j^{+}_{q_{A}}j^{-}_{q_{B}}}

by extending the rational maps
\left.\left(F_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\circ e\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{A0}\circ\mathcal{R}_{A0B0}\circ\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)}
. Composing isomorphisms (
27
), (
46
), (
47
), we can conclude by (
49
) that
\mathcal{L}
is the same as the morphism
(l_{1},l_{2})
from

(51)

\left(\mathbb{A}^{l}\times\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}\right)

to
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}\times{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}[t]
, which is
defined as follows.

l_{1}
is the projection from (
51
) to
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}
;
l_{2}:=l_{t}\circ p
where
p
is the projection from (
51
) to
\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}
, and
l_{t}:\mathbb{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{A}_{-}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}[t]
is induced by the ring homomorphism
t\mapsto\epsilon_{1}^{+}\epsilon_{1}^{-}
. Then, it is clear that the following holds.

\bullet

The image of
\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)
under the morphism
\mathcal{L}
has a reduced scheme structure as a locally closed subscheme of (
50
), which we denote by
\mathbb{M}
.

\bullet

\mathbb{M}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}\times{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}[t]
, and hence it is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimension
n-3
.

\bullet

The morphism induced by
\mathcal{L}
from

\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)
to
\mathbb{M}
is flat, which we also denote by
\mathcal{L}
by a slight abuse of notation.

Recall that by Claim
3.13.2
the restrictions of the rational maps

\chi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}:=\left.\left(F_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\circ e\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{A0}\circ\mathcal{R}_{A0B0}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{AB}\right)\right|_{\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\cap\left({\mathcal{R}}_{A0}\circ{\mathcal{R}}_{A0B0}\right)^{-1}\left({\mathring{A}^{\tau}}\right)\right)}

extends to a morphism for any
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n
. Now, to prove (
P2
) it suffices to show that
\chi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
for indices in (C1)-(C8) further factor through
\mathcal{L}:\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
.

We may identify
\left(\mathcal{R}_{AB}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}}\right)
with (
51
) via isomorphisms (
27
), (
46
), (
47
), and identify
\mathbb{M}
with
\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{+}}\times\mathbb{M}^{\lambda^{-}}\times\mathbb{A}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\lambda^{+}
and
\lambda^{-}
are the identity permutations, and
j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}<j^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}<\cdots<j^{+}_{\alpha_{l}}<j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}<j^{-}_{\beta_{2}}<\cdots<j^{-}_{\beta_{m}}
. According to (
44
),
\chi_{12j^{+}_{\alpha_{p}}j^{+}_{\alpha_{p+1}}}
,
1\leq p\leq l-1
, is induced by the ring homomorphism

z_{12j^{+}_{\alpha_{p}}j^{+}_{\alpha_{p+1}}}\left(z_{1j^{+}_{\alpha_{p}}2j^{+}_{\alpha_{p+1}}}\right)^{-1}\mapsto 1-\epsilon^{+}_{p+1},\,\,\,\,z_{1j^{+}_{\alpha_{p+1}}2j^{+}_{\alpha_{p}}}\left(z_{1j^{+}_{\alpha_{p}}2j^{+}_{\alpha_{p+1}}}\right)^{-1}\mapsto\epsilon^{+}_{p+1},

and
\chi_{12j^{-}_{\beta_{q}}j^{-}_{\beta_{q+1}}}
,
1\leq q\leq m-1
, is induced by the ring homomorphism

z_{12j^{-}_{\beta_{q}}j^{-}_{\beta_{q+1}}}\left(z_{1j^{-}_{\beta_{q}}2j^{-}_{\beta_{q+1}}}\right)^{-1}\mapsto 1-\epsilon^{-}_{q+1},\,\,\,\,z_{1j^{-}_{\beta_{q+1}}2j^{-}_{\beta_{q}}}\left(z_{1j^{-}_{\beta_{q}}2j^{-}_{\beta_{q+1}}}\right)^{-1}\mapsto\epsilon^{-}_{q+1},\\

where
\left[z_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{3}i_{2}i_{4}},z_{i_{1}i_{4}i_{2}i_{3}}\right]
are homogeneous coordinates for
\mathbb{P}^{N_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}}
. Then it suffices to show that for indices in (C1)-(C8), each component of
\chi_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
is a polynomial in
\left(\epsilon^{+}_{1}\epsilon^{-}_{1}\right)
,
\epsilon^{+}_{2},\epsilon^{+}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{l}
, and
\epsilon^{-}_{2},\epsilon^{-}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{-}_{m}
. Computation yields that for
1\leq\alpha_{1}\leq l
and
1\leq\beta_{1}\leq m
,
\chi_{12j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}
is induced by the ring homomorphism

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{z_{12j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}}{z_{1j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}2j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}}\mapsto\frac{a_{j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}a_{j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}-a_{j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}a_{j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}\left(\epsilon_{1}^{+}\epsilon^{-}_{1}\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\beta_{1}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}}{-a_{j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}}=-1+\left(\epsilon_{1}^{+}\epsilon^{-}_{1}\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\beta_{1}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}\\
&\frac{z_{1j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}2j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}}{z_{1j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}2j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}}\mapsto\frac{-a_{j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}a_{j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}\left(\epsilon_{1}^{+}\epsilon^{-}_{1}\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\beta_{1}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}}{-a_{j^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}\cdot a_{j^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}}=\left(\epsilon_{1}^{+}\epsilon^{-}_{1}\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\alpha_{1}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{\beta_{1}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}\\
\end{aligned}.\right.

The same result holds for indices in (C2)-(C7), and we omit the details here for brevity.

We complete the proof of Lemma
3.12
.

3.5.
Induction for Type (
2
)

In this subsection, we shall prove that

Lemma 3.14
.

Suppose that Proposition
3.1
holds for all integers
N^{\prime}
such that
3\leq N^{\prime}<N
. Then Lemma
3.11
holds for all (truncated) coordinate charts of
Type (
2
)
.

Proof of Lemma
3.14
.
Without loss of generality, we assume

\tau=\left(1,2,(j_{1}^{+},\cdots,j^{+}_{N-2})\right)
.

Let
B^{\lambda}\subset\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right)
be the open subscheme associated to any permutation
\lambda
as in Lemma
3.8
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\lambda
is the identity permutation.
Denote
\mathbb{A}_{+}:={\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{2},\epsilon^{+}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\right]
. Define a morphism
j:\mathbb{A}_{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(2,n)
by

(52)

\left(\begin{matrix}1&0&1&1&1&\cdots&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&1&\epsilon^{+}_{2}&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{k}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\end{matrix}\right).

According to Definition
2.12
, we can define a blow-up
\mathcal{R}^{\underline{s}}_{j}:\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}}_{j}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{+}
with respect to the product of ideal sheaves
\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}
. Similarly to (
46
), we can prove that

(53)

\left(\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(B^{\lambda}\right)\cong{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A}\right]\times{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{1}\right]\times\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}}_{j}.

As in §
2.2
, we will interpret
\mathcal{R}^{\underline{s}}_{j}
as a sequence of blow-ups. We partition (
15
) as follows.

(E0)

\{(1,2,i_{3},i_{4})|\,\,3\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(E1)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,\,3\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(E2)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,\,3\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(E3)

\{(2,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,\,3\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

Define a total order
\sigma
so that the
4
-tuples in an upper subset are strictly smaller than the ones in a lower subset.
For
0\leq k<\frac{n!}{24(n-4)!}
, we inductively define
\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{k+1}:\check{W}^{\sigma}_{k+1}\rightarrow\check{W}^{\sigma}_{k}
to be the blow-up of
\check{W}^{\sigma}_{k}
with respect to
\left(\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{k}\right)^{-1}\left(\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\check{W}^{\sigma}_{k}}
, where
\check{W}^{\sigma}_{0}=\mathbb{A}_{+}
, and
\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}
are identified with
\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
as in §
3.2
.

For simplicity, we denote

\begin{split}&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{\varheart}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{2}\circ\cdots\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-2)!}{24\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{6\cdot(n-5)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{2\cdot(n-4)!}},\\
&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{\vardiamond}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-2)!}{24\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{6\cdot(n-5)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{2\cdot(n-4)!}+1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-2)!}{24\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{6\cdot(n-5)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{2\cdot(n-4)!}+2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{n!}{24\cdot(n-4)!}}.\\
\end{split}

It is clear that
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{\vardiamond}
is the sequence of blow-ups with respect to the (pull-backs of) ideal sheaves
\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
with indices in (E3). Similarly to Claim
3.13.2
, we can show that
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{\vardiamond}
is an isomorphism.

Define a morphism
L:\mathbb{A}_{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(2,n-1)
by

(54)

\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&1&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&\epsilon^{+}_{2}&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\end{matrix}\right).

Denote

\underline{s}^{*}=(1,1,\cdots,1)\in\mathbb{Z}^{N-1}.

Then, it is clear that

(55)

\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}}_{j}\cong\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{\varheart}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbb{A}_{+}\right)\cong\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{L},

where
{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{L}:\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{L}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{+}
is the blow-up given by Definition
2.12
.

Define an open cover
\left\{C^{k}_{N}\right\}_{k=2}^{N-1}
of
\mathbb{A}_{+}
by

(56)

C^{k}_{N}:=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbb{A}_{+}\left|\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\notin\mathfrak{p},\,\forall\,2\leq\gamma\leq k-1,\,\,{\rm and}\,\,1-\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=j_{1}}^{j_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\notin\mathfrak{p},\,\forall\,2\leq j_{1}\leq k\leq j_{2}\leq N-2\right.\right\}.

Then, to prove Lemma
3.14
it suffices to show that

(Q1)

for each
2\leq k\leq N-1
,
\left({\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{L}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{k}_{N}\right)
is a locally closed subscheme of
\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
, and is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimension
n-3
.

We proceed to prove
(
Q1
)
holds on a case by case basis.

Case I (
k=N-1
).
Define a morphism
J
from
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\cdots,\xi_{N-1}\right]
to
\mathcal{G}(2,n-1)
by

(57)

\left(\begin{matrix}1&0&\xi_{3}&\xi_{4}&\cdots&\xi_{n-1}\\
0&1&1&1&\cdots&1\end{matrix}\right).

Define an open subscheme
D
of
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\cdots,\xi_{N-1}\right]
by

D:=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\cdots,\xi_{N-1}\right]\big|\,\xi_{k}+1\notin\mathfrak{p},\,\,\forall\,3\leq k\leq N-1\right\}.

Applying the change of coordinates

\begin{split}\xi_{k}:=\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\cdots\epsilon_{k-1}\right)^{-1}-1,\,\,\,\,\,\,\forall\,3\leq k\leq N-1,\end{split}

we can derive that

\begin{split}D&={\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\xi_{3}+1,\cdots,\xi_{N-1}+1,\left(\xi_{3}+1\right)^{-1},\cdots,\left(\xi_{N-1}+1\right)^{-1}\right]\\
&\cong{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon_{2},\cdots,\epsilon_{N-2},\epsilon_{2}^{-1},\cdots,\epsilon_{N-2}^{-1}\right]=C^{N-1}_{N}.\\
\end{split}

Combining (
54
), (
57
) and the fact that

\begin{split}&\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&\epsilon^{+}_{2}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)\cdot\left(\begin{matrix}1&0&\frac{1}{\epsilon^{+}_{2}}-1&\cdots&\frac{1}{\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}}-1\\
0&1&1&\cdots&1\\
\end{matrix}\right)\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\cdot{\rm diag}\left(1,1,\epsilon^{+}_{2},\epsilon^{+}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\right),\\
\end{split}

we can conclude that

\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\left(\pi\circ L\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{C^{N-1}_{N}}\cong a\cdot\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\left(\pi\circ J\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{D},

where
a
is a non-zero element of
\Gamma\left(D,\mathcal{O}_{D}\right)
. Then,
\left({\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{L}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{N-1}_{N}\right)
is isomorphic to an open subscheme of
\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{J}
.

Now we consider the restriction of the blow-up
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{\underline{s}^{*}}:\mathcal{T}_{n-1}^{\underline{s}^{*}}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{n-1}^{\underline{s}^{*}}
to
\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{\underline{s}^{*}}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau_{-}}\right)
, where
\tau_{-}=\left(1,2,(j_{1}^{-},\cdots,j^{-}_{N-3})\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}^{*}}
.
Similarly to the proof of (
46
), one can show that

\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{{\underline{s}^{*}}}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau_{-}}\right)\cong{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A}\right]\times\mathbb{M}^{\underline{s}^{*}}_{\widetilde{J}}.

By the assumption that
\mathcal{T}_{n-1}^{\underline{s}^{*}}
is smooth,
we can complete the proof for case I.

Case II (
k=2
).

Partition the set

(58)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}|\,3\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}\cup\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}|\,3\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}

as follows.

(G0)

\{(1,3,i_{3},i_{4})|4\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(G1)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(G2)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|4\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(G3)

\{(3,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

Define a total order
\sigma
so that the
4
-tuples in an upper subset are strictly smaller than the ones in a lower subset.
Corresponding to indices in (G0), (G1), (G2), we define

\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,1}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-3)!}{24\cdot(n-7)!}+\frac{(n-3)!}{6\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-3)(n-4)}{2}},

and corresponding to indices in (G3), we define

\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,2}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-3)!}{24\cdot(n-7)!}+\frac{(n-3)!}{6\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-3)(n-4)}{2}+1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-3)!}{24\cdot(n-7)!}+\frac{(n-3)!}{6\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-3)(n-4)}{2}+2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-3)!}{24\cdot(n-7)!}+\frac{(n-3)!}{3\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-3)(n-4)}{2}}.

Computation yields that for
4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n
,
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{1i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{C^{2}_{N}}
is a product of the invertible ideal sheaf generated by
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\cdot\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}
and the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{1i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
generated by

(59)

\left\{\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{3}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1,\,\,\,\,\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1,\,\,\,\,\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right\}.

For
4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n
,
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{3i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{C^{2}_{N}}
is generated by

\begin{split}&\left\{\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{3}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\,\,\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right\}.\\
\end{split}

Since
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{\alpha}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
in
C^{2}_{N}
for
\alpha=2,3,4
, we can derive that
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{3i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{C^{2}_{N}}
is a product of
\mathcal{I}_{1i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
and the invertible ideal sheaf generated by
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}
.
Then the restriction of
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,2}
to

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,1}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{2}_{N}\right)
is an isomorphism. Hence to prove (
Q1
) for
k=2
, it suffices to show that
\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,1}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbb{A}_{+}\right)
is smooth.

Define a morphism
\widetilde{L}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{3},\epsilon^{+}_{4},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\right]\rightarrow\mathcal{G}(2,n-2)
by

(60)

\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&1&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&\epsilon^{+}_{3}&\epsilon^{+}_{3}\epsilon^{+}_{4}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{3}\epsilon^{+}_{4}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\end{matrix}\right).

One can show that

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{2,1}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathbb{A}_{+}\right)\cong{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{2}\right]\times\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widehat{s}}}_{\widetilde{L}},

where
\underline{\widehat{s}}=(1,1,\cdots,1)\in\mathbb{Z}^{N-2}
. By the assumption that Proposition
3.1
holds for all
N^{\prime}
such that
4\leq N^{\prime}<N
, we can conclude
by (
53
), (
54
), (
55
) that
\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widehat{s}}}_{\widetilde{L}}
is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
.

The proof is complete.

Case III (
3\leq k\leq N-2
).
Partition set (
58
) as follows.

(A1)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,k+2\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(A2)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,k+2\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(B1)

\{(1,3,i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq k+1\}
.

(B2)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq k+1\}
.

(B3)

\{(3,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq k+1\}
.

(B4)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq k+1\}
.

(C1)

\{(1,3,i_{3},i_{4})|\,k+2\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C2)

\{(3,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,k+2\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C3)

\{(1,3,i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{3}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C4)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C5)

\{(1,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C6)

\{(3,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C7)

\{(3,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{2}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C8)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C9)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{1}<i_{2}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

(C10)

\{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})|\,4\leq i_{1}\leq k+1,\,\,k+2\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n\}
.

Define a total order
\sigma
so that the
4
-tuples in an upper subset are strictly smaller than the ones in a lower subset.
Define

\begin{split}&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}},\\
&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}+1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}+2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}+N_{2}},\\
&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,3}:=\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}+N_{2}+1}\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{N_{1}+N_{2}+2}\circ\cdots\circ\check{\psi}^{\sigma}_{\frac{(n-2)!}{24\cdot(n-6)!}+\frac{(n-2)!}{6\cdot(n-5)!}},\\
\end{split}

where
N_{1}
(resp.
N_{2}
) is the sum of the cardinalities of (A1)-(A2) (resp. (B1)-(B4)). Equivalently,
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}
(
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}
) is the sequence of blow-ups with respect to the (pull-backs of) ideal sheaves
\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
with indices in (A1)-(A2) (resp. (B1)-(B4)).

Claim 3.14.1
.

The restriction of
\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,3}
to

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}\circ\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{k}_{N}\right)
is an isomorphism.

Proof of Claim
3.14.1
.
The proof is similar to that for Claim
3.13.2
. It suffices to show that for any
(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})
in (C1)-(C10), the restriction to

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}\circ\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{k}_{N}\right)
of the ideal sheaf

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}\circ\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}\right)^{-1}\left(\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}\circ\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}\right)^{-1}\left(C^{k}_{N}\right)}=:\mathscr{I}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}

is invertible.
The argument is on a case by case basis.

In case (C1),
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{13i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}
,
k+2\leq i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n
, is generated by

\left\{\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{3}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\,\,\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\,\,\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right\}.

Since
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
and
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
in
C^{k}_{N}
,
\mathscr{I}_{13i_{3}i_{4}}
is generated by
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}
. Similarly, we can show that
\mathscr{I}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
are invertible for indices in (C3), (C4), (C5), (C7), (C9).

In case (C2),
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{3i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}
,
k+2\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq n
, is generated by

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{3}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\,\,\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\left(\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right)\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right\}.

Computation yields that
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{1i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}
,
4\leq i_{2}<i_{3}<i_{4}\leq k+1
, is a product of the invertible ideal sheaf generated by
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\cdot\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}
and the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
generated by

\left\{\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{3}+1}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1,\,\,\,\,\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=i_{2}+1}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\right\}.

Since
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
,
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{3}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
,
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{4}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}-1\neq 0
in
C^{k}_{N}
, we can derive that
\left(\pi\circ j\right)^{-1}\mathscr{I}^{\underline{s}}_{3i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}_{+}}
is a product of the invertible ideal sheaf generated by
\prod\nolimits_{\gamma=2}^{i_{2}}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}
and the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
, and hence
\mathscr{I}_{3i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
is invertible. Similarly, we can show that
\mathscr{I}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}
are invertible for indices in (C6), (C8), (C10).

We complete the proof of Claim
3.14.1
.

Define an open subscheme of
\mathbb{A}_{+}
by

D^{k}_{N}:=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathbb{A}_{+}\big|\,\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\notin\mathfrak{p},\,\,\forall\,2\leq\gamma\leq k-1\right\}
.
Similarly to (
60
), we define a morphism
\widetilde{L}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{2},\epsilon^{+}_{3},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{k-1}\right]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}(2,k)
by

\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&1&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&\epsilon^{+}_{2}&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{2}\epsilon^{+}_{3}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{k-1}\end{matrix}\right),

and a morphism
\widehat{L}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{k+1},\epsilon^{+}_{k+2},\cdots,\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\right]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}(2,n-k)
by

\left(\begin{matrix}1&1&1&1&\cdots&1\\
0&1&\epsilon^{+}_{k+1}&\epsilon^{+}_{k+1}\epsilon^{+}_{k+2}&\cdots&\epsilon^{+}_{k+1}\epsilon^{+}_{k+2}\cdots\epsilon^{+}_{N-2}\end{matrix}\right).

Now by Claim
3.14.1
, we can show that

\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,1}\circ\check{\mathcal{R}}_{3,2}\right)^{-1}\left(D^{k}_{N}\right)
is isomorphic to an open subscheme of
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\epsilon^{+}_{k}\right]\times\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widetilde{s}}}_{\widetilde{L}}\times\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widehat{s}}}_{\widehat{L}}
,
where
\underline{\widetilde{s}}=(1,1,\cdots,1)\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,
\underline{\widehat{s}}=(1,1,\cdots,1)\in\mathbb{Z}^{N-k}
. By the same argument as that in case II, we can derive that
\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widetilde{s}}}_{\widetilde{L}}
,
\mathbb{M}^{\underline{\widehat{s}}}_{\widehat{L}}
are smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
. We can thus conclude the proof of (
Q1
) for case III.

The proof of Lemma
3.14
is complete.

4.
The Case of Sub-torus Actions

In this section, we are concerned with

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
N\geq 2
be an integer. Let
\underline{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{N})
be a size vector. Denote
n:=s_{1}+s_{2}+\cdots s_{N}
. Then,
\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
are smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimensions
2n-4
and
2n-N-3
, respectively.
Moreover, the morphism
\mathcal{P}^{\underline{s}}_{n}:\mathcal{T}_{n}^{\underline{s}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
is flat.

We note that the structure of the section is in parallel with that of §
3
to closely following the ideas therein, while the proofs will be concise to avoid repetition.

4.1.
Parametrizations for
\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}

To better locate relative positions of columns, we introduce

Definition 4.2
.

Fix a size vector
\underline{s}=(s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{N})
. We associate to each integer
1\leq j\leq n
a triple of positive integers
\left(j,t,p\right)
such that

(1)

1\leq t\leq N
,

(2)

1\leq p\leq s_{t}
,

(3)

j=s_{1}+s_{2}+\cdots+s_{t-1}+p
.

Here we adopt the convention that
s_{0}=0
. We call
\left(j,t,p\right)
the
\underline{s}
-triple of
j
. We also write
\left(j,t,p\right)
as
j
for simplicity of notation when there is no ambiguity.

We now define

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
be the index set consisting of all tuples of
\underline{s}
-triples

(61)

\small\left(\left(j_{1},t_{1},p_{1}\right),\left(j_{2},t_{2},p_{2}\right),\left(\left(j^{+}_{1},t^{+}_{1},p^{+}_{1}\right)\cdots,\left(j^{+}_{l},t^{+}_{l},p^{+}_{l}\right)\right),\left(\left(j^{-}_{1},t^{-}_{1},p^{-}_{1}\right),\cdots,\left(j^{-}_{m},t^{-}_{m},p^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\right)

with the following properties.

(1)

l,m
are non-negative integers such that
l+m=N-2
or
N-1
. Moreover, if
l+m=N-2
, then
t_{1}<t_{2}
; if
l+m=N-1
, then
t_{1}=t_{2}
and
p_{1}<p_{2}
.

(2)

t^{+}_{1}<t^{+}_{2}<\cdots<t^{+}_{l}
,
t^{-}_{1}<t^{-}_{2}<\cdots<t^{-}_{m}
, and
\{t_{1},t_{2},t^{+}_{1},\cdots,t^{+}_{l},t^{-}_{1},\cdots,t^{-}_{m}\}=\{1,2,\cdots,N\}
.

We write
(
61
) as
\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)
for simplicity of notation. We call an index
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
a Class I index if
l+m=N-2
, and a Class II index if
l+m=N-1
.

We associate to each Class I index
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
an affine space
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{Y},\overrightarrow{Z},\overrightarrow{U},\overrightarrow{V},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\overrightarrow{H}^{2},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{2},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]
. Here

(62)

\begin{split}&\,\overrightarrow{A}:=\left(a_{j^{+}_{1}},a_{j^{+}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{+}_{l}},a_{j^{-}_{1}},a_{j^{-}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{-}_{m}}\right);\\
\end{split}

(63)

\begin{split}&\overrightarrow{Y}:=\left(y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{p_{1}-1},y_{p_{1}+1},\cdots,y_{s_{t_{1}}}\right),\,\,\overrightarrow{U}:=\left(u_{1},u_{2},\cdots,u_{p_{1}-1},u_{p_{1}+1},\cdots,u_{s_{t_{1}}}\right),\\
&\overrightarrow{Z}:=\left(z_{1},z_{2},\cdots,z_{p_{2}-1},z_{p_{2}+1},\cdots,z_{s_{t_{2}}}\right),\,\,\overrightarrow{V}:=\left(v_{1},v_{2},\cdots,v_{p_{2}-1},v_{p_{2}+1},\cdots,v_{s_{t_{2}}}\right);\\
\end{split}

for
1\leq\alpha\leq l
,

(64)

\overrightarrow{H}^{\alpha}:=\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{11},\eta^{\alpha}_{12},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{1\left(p_{\alpha}^{+}-1\right)},\eta^{\alpha}_{1\left(p_{\alpha}^{+}+1\right)},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{1s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}},\eta^{\alpha}_{21},\eta^{\alpha}_{22},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right);

for
1\leq\beta\leq m
,

(65)

\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{\beta}:=\left(\xi^{\beta}_{11},\xi^{\beta}_{12},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}},\xi^{\beta}_{21},\xi^{\beta}_{22},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{2\left(p_{\beta}^{-}-1\right)},\xi^{\beta}_{2\left(p_{\beta}^{-}+1\right)},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{2s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right).

Remark 4.4
.

We adopt the convention that there is no such variable when the subscript is out of range. For instance, there is no
y_{p_{1}-1}
,
u_{p_{1}-1}
when
p_{1}=1
,
and no
y_{p_{1}+1}
,
u_{p_{1}+1}
when
p_{1}=s_{t_{1}}
.

We next define a morphism
\Gamma^{\tau}
from

(66)

{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{Y},\overrightarrow{Z},\overrightarrow{U},\overrightarrow{V},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\overrightarrow{H}^{2},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{2},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]

to
U_{j_{1}j_{2}}\subset G(2,n)
as follows. For
j_{1}-p_{1}+1\leq i\leq j_{1}-p_{1}+s_{t_{1}}
, and
i\neq j_{1}
, set

(67)

x_{1i}\mapsto y_{\left(i-j_{1}+p_{1}\right)},\,\,\,x_{2i}\mapsto u_{\left(i-j_{1}+p_{1}\right)};

for
j_{2}-p_{2}+1\leq i\leq j_{2}-p_{2}+s_{t_{2}}
, and
i\neq j_{2}
, set

(68)

x_{1i}\mapsto v_{\left(i-j_{2}+p_{2}\right)},\,\,\,x_{2i}\mapsto z_{\left(i-j_{2}+p_{2}\right)};

for
1\leq\alpha\leq l
, set

(69)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &x_{1j_{\alpha}^{+}}\mapsto a_{j_{\alpha}^{+}},\\
&x_{1i}\mapsto a_{j_{\alpha}^{+}}\cdot\eta^{\alpha}_{1\left(i-j_{\alpha}^{+}+p_{\alpha}^{+}\right)},&j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+1\leq i\leq j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}\,\,{\rm and}\,\,i\neq j_{\alpha}^{+}\\
&x_{2i}\mapsto a_{j_{\alpha}^{+}}\cdot\eta^{\alpha}_{2\left(i-j_{\alpha}^{+}+p_{\alpha}^{+}\right)},&j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+1\leq i\leq j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\\
\end{aligned}\right.\,\,;

for
1\leq\beta\leq m
, set

(70)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &x_{1i}\mapsto a_{j_{\beta}^{-}}\cdot\xi^{\beta}_{1\left(i-j_{\beta}^{-}+p_{\beta}^{-}\right)},&j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+1\leq i\leq j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\\
&x_{2j_{\beta}^{-}}\mapsto a_{j_{\beta}^{-}},\\
&x_{2i}\mapsto a_{j_{\beta}^{-}}\cdot\xi^{\beta}_{2\left(i-j_{\beta}^{-}+p_{\beta}^{-}\right)},&j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+1\leq i\leq j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}\,\,{\rm and}\,\,i\neq j_{\beta}^{-}\\
\end{aligned}\right.\,\,.

Similarly, we associate to each Class II index
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
an affine space
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{W},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]
. Here
\overrightarrow{A}
are defined by (

);
\overrightarrow{H}^{\alpha}
are defined by (

) for

;
\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{\beta}
are defined by (

) for

;

(71)

\begin{split}&\overrightarrow{W}:=\left(w_{11},w_{12},\cdots,w_{1(p_{1}-1)},w_{1(p_{1}+1)},\cdots,w_{1(p_{2}-1)},w_{1(p_{2}+1)},\cdots,w_{s_{t_{1}}},\right.\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left.w_{21},w_{22},\cdots,w_{2(p_{1}-1)},w_{2(p_{1}+1)},\cdots,w_{2(p_{2}-1)},w_{2(p_{2}+1)},\cdots,w_{2s_{t_{1}}}\right).\\
\end{split}

Define a morphism
\Gamma^{\tau}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{W},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]\longrightarrow U_{j_{1}j_{2}}
as follows. For
j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+1\leq i\leq j_{\alpha}^{+}-p_{\alpha}^{+}+s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}
where
1\leq\alpha\leq l
, define the images of
x_{1i}
,
x_{2i}
by (
69
); for
j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+1\leq i\leq j_{\beta}^{-}-p_{\beta}^{-}+s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}
where
1\leq\beta\leq m
, define the images of
x_{1i}
,
x_{2i}
by (
70
).
For
j_{1}-p_{1}+1\leq i\leq j_{1}-p_{1}+s_{t_{1}}
, and
i\neq j_{1},j_{2}
, define

(72)

x_{1i}\mapsto w_{1\left(i-j_{1}+p_{1}\right)},\,\,\,x_{2i}\mapsto w_{2\left(i-j_{1}+p_{1}\right)}.

Similarly to Lemma
3.3
, we can define for each Class I index
\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
a locally closed embedding

J^{\tau}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{Y},\overrightarrow{Z},\overrightarrow{U},\overrightarrow{V},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}

and for each Class II index
\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
a locally closed embedding

J^{\tau}:{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{W},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}

by extend the rational maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\underline{s}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}=\left(e\circ\Gamma^{\tau},F^{\underline{s}}_{1}\circ e\circ\Gamma^{\tau},F^{\underline{s}}_{2}\circ e\circ\Gamma^{\tau},\cdots,F^{\underline{s}}_{N}\circ e\circ\Gamma^{\tau}\right)
. Denote by
A^{\tau}
the corresponding image under
J^{\tau}
for each
\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
, which is equipped with the reduced scheme structure as a locally closed subscheme of
\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}
. Moreover,

Proposition 4.5
.

\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
is a union of
A^{\tau}
for all
\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
.
\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.5
.
The proof is similar to that of Lemma
3.4
and Corollary
3.5
. We omit the details for brevity.

4.2.
Factorization

Fix a class I index
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
. In what follows, we shall write
{\underline{w}}\in C^{\underline{s}}
as

(73)

\underline{w}=(w_{t_{1}},w_{t_{2}},w_{t^{+}_{1}},\cdots,w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}},\cdots,w_{t^{+}_{l}},w_{t^{-}_{1}},\cdots,w_{t^{-}_{\beta}},\cdots,w_{t^{-}_{m}}),

by rearranging its components corresponding to
\tau
.

Let (
{\rm AB}{\tau}
) be the subset of
C^{\underline{s}}
consisting of the following elements.

(A0)

w_{t_{1}}=2
, and the other components are
0
.

(A1)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}}=1
for a certain
1\leq\alpha\leq l
, and the other components are
0
.

(A2)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=1
for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}\leq l
.

(B0)

w_{t_{2}}=2
, and the other components are
0
.

(B1)

w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
for a certain
1\leq\beta\leq m
, and the other components are
0
.

(B2)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=1
for certain
1\leq\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}\leq m
.

Denote by
N_{\tau}
the cardinality of subset (
{\rm AB}{\tau}
). Take a total order
\sigma
on
C^{\underline{s}}
so that
\sigma(\gamma)
are in subset
{\rm(AB}{\tau}{\rm)}
for
1\leq\gamma\leq N_{\tau}
.
As in §
2.2
,
we define

(74)

\begin{split}&\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}:=\psi^{\sigma}_{1}\circ\psi^{\sigma}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ\psi^{\sigma}_{N_{\tau}},\\
&\ddot{\mathcal{R}}_{N}:=\psi^{\sigma}_{N_{\tau}+1}\circ\psi^{\sigma}_{N_{\tau}+2}\circ\cdots\circ\psi^{\sigma}_{N_{\underline{s}}}.\\
\end{split}

To describe an open cover of
\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right)
, we define
\Lambda^{\tau}
to be the index set consisting of indices
\lambda:=\left(q^{+},q^{-},\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-},\Delta^{+},\Delta^{-}\right)
with the following properties.

(1)

q^{+}
and
q^{-}
are integers such that

\bullet

0\leq q^{+}\leq l
and
0\leq q^{-}\leq m
,

\bullet

if
s_{t_{1}}=1
and
s_{t_{2}}=1
, then
q^{+}=l
and
q^{-}=m
, respectively.

(2)

\lambda^{+}=(\lambda^{+}_{1},\lambda^{+}_{2},\cdots,\lambda^{+}_{l})
is a permutation of
\{1,2,\cdots,l\}
,
and
\lambda^{-}=(\lambda^{-}_{1},\lambda^{-}_{2},\cdots,\lambda^{-}_{m})
is a permutation of
\{1,2,\cdots,m\}
.

(3)

\Delta^{+}:=\left(\delta^{+}_{1},\cdots,\delta^{+}_{\rho},\cdots,\delta^{+}_{q^{+}},\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}\right)
is a
\left(q^{+}+1\right)
-tuple of integers such that

\bullet

1\leq\delta^{+}_{\rho}\leq s_{t^{+}_{\lambda^{+}_{\rho}}}
, for
1\leq\rho\leq q^{+}
,

\bullet

1\leq\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}\leq s_{t_{1}}
and
\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}\neq p_{1}
.

\Delta^{-}:=\left(\delta_{1}^{-},\cdots,\delta_{\rho}^{-},\cdots,\delta_{q^{-}}^{-},\delta_{q^{-}+1}^{-}\right)
is a
(q^{-}+1)
-tuple of integers such that

\bullet

1\leq\delta^{-}_{\rho}\leq s_{t^{-}_{\lambda^{-}_{\rho}}}
, for
1\leq\rho\leq q^{-}
,

\bullet

1\leq\delta_{q^{-}+1}^{-}\leq s_{t_{2}}
and
\delta_{q^{-}+1}^{-}\neq p_{2}
.

Remark 4.6
.

We also adopt the following conventions.

\bullet

When
s_{t_{1}}=1
(resp.
s_{t_{2}}=1
), there is no
\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}
(resp.
\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}
).

\bullet

If
l=0
(resp.
m=0
), then
\lambda^{+}
and
\Delta^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
and
\Delta^{-}
) are null, and we write
\lambda=\left(q^{+},q^{-},\lambda^{-},\Delta^{-}\right)
(resp.
\lambda=\left(q^{+},q^{-},\lambda^{+},\Delta^{+}\right)
).

We associate to each
\lambda=\left(q^{+},q^{-},\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-},\Delta^{+},\Delta^{-}\right)\in\Lambda^{\tau}
an affine space

(75)

\small{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{Y},\overrightarrow{Z},\overrightarrow{E}_{+},\overrightarrow{E}_{-},\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}},\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{V}},\overrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}^{l},\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}^{l},\overrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}^{m},\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{m}\right].

Here

(76)

\begin{split}&\overrightarrow{A}:=\left(a_{j^{+}_{1}},a_{j^{+}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{+}_{l}},a_{j^{-}_{1}},a_{j^{-}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{-}_{m}}\right),\\
&\overrightarrow{Y}:=\left(y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{p_{1}-1},y_{p_{1}+1},\cdots,y_{s_{t_{1}}}\right),\,\,\overrightarrow{Z}:=\left(z_{1},z_{2},\cdots,z_{p_{2}-1},z_{p_{2}+1},\cdots,z_{s_{t_{2}}}\right),\\
&\overrightarrow{E}_{+}:=\left(\epsilon_{1}^{+},\epsilon_{2}^{+},\cdots,\epsilon_{q^{+}}^{+},\epsilon_{q^{+}+1}^{+}\right),\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\overrightarrow{E}_{-}:=\left(\epsilon_{1}^{-},\epsilon_{2}^{-},\cdots,\epsilon_{q^{-}}^{-},\epsilon^{-}_{q^{-}+1}\right),\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}&\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}}:=\left(\mathfrak{u}_{1},\mathfrak{u}_{2},\cdots,\mathfrak{u}_{p_{1}-1},\mathfrak{u}_{p_{1}+1},\cdots,\mathfrak{u}_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}-1},\mathfrak{u}_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}+1},\cdots,\mathfrak{u}_{s_{1}}\right),\\
&\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{V}}:=\left(\mathfrak{v}_{1},\mathfrak{v}_{2},\cdots,\mathfrak{v}_{p_{2}-1},\mathfrak{v}_{p_{2}+1},\cdots,\mathfrak{v}_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}-1},\mathfrak{v}_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}+1},\cdots,\mathfrak{v}_{s_{2}}\right);\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\\
\end{split}

for
1\leq\alpha\leq l
,

(77)

\begin{split}&\overrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}^{\alpha}:=\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{11},\eta^{\alpha}_{12},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{1\left(p_{\alpha}^{+}-1\right)},\eta^{\alpha}_{1\left(p_{\alpha}^{+}+1\right)},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{1s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right);\\
\end{split}

for
\alpha=\lambda^{+}_{\rho}
where
1\leq\rho\leq q^{+}
,

(78)

\overrightarrow{{\mathfrak{H}}}^{\alpha}:=\left({\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{21},{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{22},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}-1\right)},{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}+1\right)},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right),

and for
\alpha=\lambda^{+}_{\rho}
where
q^{+}+1\leq\rho\leq l
,

(79)

\overrightarrow{{\mathfrak{H}}}^{\alpha}:=\left({\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{21},{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{22},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right);

for
1\leq\beta\leq m
,

(80)

\overrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}^{\beta}:=\left(\xi^{\beta}_{21},\xi^{\beta}_{22},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{2\left(p_{\beta}^{-}-1\right)},\xi^{\beta}_{2\left(p_{\beta}^{-}+1\right)},\cdots,\xi^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right);

for
\beta=\lambda^{-}_{\rho}
where
1\leq\rho\leq q^{-}
,

(81)

\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{\beta}:=\left({\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{11},{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{12},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}-1\right)},{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}+1\right)},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right),

and for
\beta=\lambda^{-}_{\rho}
where
q^{-}+1\leq\rho\leq m
,

(82)

\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{\beta}:=\left({\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{11},{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{12},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right).

Remark 4.7
.

We adopt the following convention.

\bullet

When
s_{t_{1}}=1
(resp.
s_{t_{1}}=1
), there are no
\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{U}}
,
\epsilon^{+}_{q^{+}+1}
(resp.
\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{V}}
,
\epsilon^{-}_{q^{-}+1}
) variables.

\bullet

When
l=0
(resp.
m=0
), there are no
a_{j^{+}_{1}},a_{j^{+}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{+}_{l}}
,
\overrightarrow{E}_{+}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}^{\alpha}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{H}}^{\alpha}
(resp.
a_{j^{-}_{1}},a_{j^{-}_{2}},\cdots,a_{j^{-}_{m}}
,
\overrightarrow{E}_{-}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}^{\beta}
,
\overrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{\beta}
) variables.

We define a morphism
\Sigma^{\lambda}
from (
75
) to

(83)

A^{\tau}\cong{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}\left[\overrightarrow{A},\overrightarrow{Y},\overrightarrow{Z},\overrightarrow{U},\overrightarrow{V},\overrightarrow{H}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{H}^{l},\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{1},\cdots,\overrightarrow{\Xi}^{m}\right]

by the following ring homomorphism.
\overrightarrow{A}
,
\overrightarrow{Y}
,
\overrightarrow{Z}
,
\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{12},\eta^{\alpha}_{13},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{1s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right)
for
1\leq\alpha\leq l
, and
\left(\xi^{\beta}_{22},\xi^{\beta}_{23},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{2s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right)
for
1\leq\beta\leq m
are mapped to the same variables of (
83
).
When
s_{t_{1}}\geq 2
,

\footnotesize\begin{split}&\left(u_{1},\cdots,u_{p_{1}-1},u_{p_{1}+1},\cdots,u_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}-1},u_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}},u_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}+1},\cdots,u_{s_{t_{1}}}\right)\mapsto\left(\mathfrak{u}_{1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,\right.\\
&\left.\mathfrak{u}_{p_{1}-1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\mathfrak{u}_{p_{1}+1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,\mathfrak{u}_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}-1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\mathfrak{u}_{\delta^{+}_{q^{+}+1}+1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,\mathfrak{u}_{s_{t_{1}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right).\\
\end{split}

When
s_{t_{2}}\geq 2
,

\footnotesize\begin{split}&\left(v_{1},\cdots,v_{p_{2}-1},v_{p_{2}+1},\cdots,v_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}-1},v_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}},v_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}+1},\cdots,v_{s_{t_{2}}}\right)\mapsto\left(\mathfrak{v}_{1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,\right.\\
&\left.\mathfrak{v}_{p_{2}-1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,\mathfrak{v}_{p_{2}+1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,\mathfrak{v}_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}-1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=0}^{q^{-}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,\mathfrak{v}_{\delta^{-}_{q^{-}+1}+1}\prod\limits_{\gamma=0}^{q^{-}}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,\mathfrak{v}_{s_{t_{2}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}\right).\\
\end{split}

For
\alpha=\lambda^{+}_{\rho}
where
1\leq\rho\leq q^{+}
,

(84)

\begin{split}&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{21},\eta^{\alpha}_{22},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}-1\right)},\eta^{\alpha}_{2\delta^{+}_{\rho}},\eta^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}+1\right)},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right)\mapsto\\
&\left({\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{21}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{22}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}-1\right)}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2\left(\delta^{+}_{\rho}+1\right)}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right),\end{split}

and for
\alpha=\lambda^{+}_{\rho}
where
q^{+}+1\leq\rho\leq l
,

(85)

\begin{split}&\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{21},\eta^{\alpha}_{22},\cdots,\eta^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\right)\mapsto\left({\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{21}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{22}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{h}}^{\alpha}_{2s_{t_{\alpha}^{+}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{+}+1}\epsilon^{+}_{\gamma}\right).\end{split}

For
\beta=\lambda^{-}_{\rho}
where
1\leq\rho\leq q^{-}
,

(86)

\begin{split}&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\left(\xi^{\beta}_{11},\xi^{\beta}_{12},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}-1\right)},\xi^{\beta}_{1\delta^{-}_{\rho}},\xi^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}+1\right)},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right)\mapsto\\
&\left({\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{11}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{12}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}-1\right)}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1\left(\delta^{-}_{\rho}+1\right)}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{\rho}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}\right),\end{split}

and for
\beta=\lambda^{-}_{\rho}
where
q^{-}+1\leq\rho\leq m
,

\begin{split}&\left(\xi^{\beta}_{11},\xi^{\beta}_{12},\cdots,\xi^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\right)\mapsto\left({\mathfrak{x}}^{\alpha}_{11}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\,\,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\alpha}_{12}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma},\cdots,{\mathfrak{x}}^{\beta}_{1s_{t_{\beta}^{-}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma=1}^{q^{-}+1}\epsilon^{-}_{\gamma}\right).\end{split}

We can define a locally closed embedding
\Omega^{\lambda}
from (
75
) to
\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}\,\,{\rm in\,\,(AB}{\tau}{\rm)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
by extending the rational map
\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\underline{s}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}\circ\Sigma^{\lambda},\left(\cdots,F^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}\circ e\circ\Gamma^{\tau}\circ\Sigma^{\lambda},\cdots\right)_{\underline{w}\,\,{\rm in\,\,(AB}{\tau}{\rm)}}\right)
in the same way as Lemma
3.3
.
Denote by
B^{\lambda}
the corresponding image under
\Omega^{\lambda}
, which has the reduced scheme structure as a locally closed subscheme of
\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right)\subset\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{w}\,\,{\rm in\,\,(AB}{\tau}{\rm)}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\tau}
,
B^{\lambda}
is an open subscheme of
\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right)
, and

\bigcup\nolimits_{{\rm all\,\,indices\,\,}\lambda\in\Lambda^{\tau}}B^{\lambda}=\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right).

In particular,
\left(\check{\mathcal{R}}_{N}\right)^{-1}\left(A^{\tau}\right)
is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.8
.
The idea is to apply the bubble sort algorithm in the same way as Lemma
3.8
. We omit the details here for brevity.

4.3.
Reduction to a local form and induction for Types (
I1
) and (
II1
)

We modify the open cover
\left\{A^{\tau}\right\}_{\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}}
as follows. For each
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},\left(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}\right),\left(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m}\right)\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
, let
\mathring{A}^{\tau}
be the open subscheme of
A^{\tau}
defined by

\left\{\,\mathfrak{p}\in A^{\tau}\left|\,1-\eta^{\alpha}_{2p^{+}_{\alpha}}\cdot\xi^{\beta}_{1p^{-}_{\beta}}\notin\mathfrak{p},\,\,\forall\,1\leq\alpha\leq l\,\,{\rm and}\,\,1\leq\beta\leq m\right.\right\}.

By the same token, we can derive

Definition-Lemma 4.9
.

\left\{\mathring{A}^{\tau}\right\}_{\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}}
is a finite open cover of
\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
. We call
\mathring{A}^{\tau}
the (truncated) coordinate charts of
\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
.

By analogy with Lemma
3.12
, we can show that Proposition
4.1
holds for a certain
N\geq 2
if the following lemma holds for the same
N
. Note that the lemma itself is an easy consequence of Proposition
4.1
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
N\geq 2
be an integer. Let
\underline{s}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
be a size vector.
For each (truncated) coordinate chart
{\mathring{A}^{\tau}}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{\underline{s}}
, there exists a finite open cover
\{T^{\tau}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
of
\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}}_{n}\right)^{-1}\left({\mathring{A}^{\tau}}\right)
such that the following holds.

(1)

Each
T^{\tau}_{\alpha}
is an open subscheme of
\left(\overline{\mathcal{R}}^{\underline{s}}_{n}\right)^{-1}\left({\mathring{A}^{\tau}}\right)
, and is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

There is a locally closed subscheme
M^{\tau}_{\alpha}
of
\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
for each
\alpha
, such that as a topological space
M^{\tau}_{\alpha}
is the image of
T^{\tau}_{\alpha}
under the projection from
\mathbb{P}^{N_{2,n}}\times\prod\nolimits_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{t}}\times\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
to
\prod\nolimits_{\underline{w}\in C^{\underline{s}}}\mathbb{P}^{N^{\underline{s}}_{\underline{w}}}
. Moreover,
M^{\tau}_{\alpha}
is smooth over
{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
of relative dimension
2n-N-3
.

(3)

The projection from
T^{\tau}_{\alpha}
to
M^{\tau}_{\alpha}
is a flat morphism.

We define the type of
\mathring{A}^{\tau}
as follows.

Type (I1)

\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
is a Class I index such that
l,m\geq 1
.

Type (I2)

\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
is a Class I index such that
m=0
or
l=0
.

Type (II1)

\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
is a Class II index such that
l,m\geq 1
.

Type (II2)

\tau\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
is a Class II index such that
m=0
or
l=0
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Suppose that Proposition
4.1
holds for all integers
N^{\prime}
such that
2\leq N^{\prime}<N
. Then Lemma
4.10
holds for all (truncated) coordinate charts of
Type (
I1
)
.

Proof of Lemma
4.11
.
Let
\tau=\left(j_{1},j_{2},(j^{+}_{1},\cdots,j^{+}_{l}),(j^{-}_{1},\cdots,j^{-}_{m})\right)\in\mathbb{J}^{\underline{s}}
be an arbitrary Class I index such that
l,m\geq 1
. We write vectors
{\underline{w}}\in C^{\underline{s}}
in form (
73
).

To define a total order
\sigma
on
C^{\underline{s}}
, we partition
C^{\underline{s}}
as follows.

(AB
\tau
)

The union of subsets (
A0
), (
A1
), (
A2
), (
B0
), (
B1
), (
B2
) defined in §
4.2
.

(AB0)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t_{2}}=1
, and the other components are
0
.

(A3)

w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}}=2
, for a certain
1\leq\alpha\leq l
, and other components are
0
.

(A4)

w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}\leq l
, and other components are
0
.

(A5)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{3}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{3}\leq l
.

(A6)

w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{3}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{4}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{3}<\alpha_{4}\leq l
.

(B3)

w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=2
, for a certain
1\leq\beta\leq m
, and other components are
0
.

(B4)

w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}\leq m
, and other components are
0
.

(B5)

w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{3}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}<\beta_{3}\leq m
.

(B6)

w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{3}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{4}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}<\beta_{3}<\beta_{4}\leq m
.

(C1)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
, for a certain
1\leq\beta\leq m
, and other components are
0
.

, for a certain

, and other components are
0
.

(C3)

, for certain

and

, and other components are
0
.

(C4)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{3}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}<\beta_{3}\leq m
.

(C5)

w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{3}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{3}\leq l
.

(C6)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha\leq l
and
1\leq\beta\leq m
.

(C7)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
, for certain

and
1\leq\beta\leq m
.

(C8)

w_{t_{1}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha\leq l
and

.

(C9)

w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
, for certain
1\leq\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}\leq l
and
1\leq\beta\leq m
.

(C10)

w_{t_{2}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=1
, for certain

and

.

(C11)

w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{1}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta_{2}}}=1
, for certain

and

(C12)

w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{1}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{2}}}=w_{t^{+}_{\alpha_{3}}}=w_{t^{-}_{\beta}}=1
, for certain
